Skip to main content Help Log in ReliefWeb Search Content Search What are you looking for? Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA DR Congo + 2 more Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak: Democratic Republic of the Congo | Uganda Weekly External Situation Report 10, Data as of 19 July 2026 Format Situation Report Source WHO Posted 28 Jul 2026 Originally published 27 Jul 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 1.14 MB) Event description Transmission of Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD) remained intense in the Democratic Republic of the Congo during the reporting week, with continued detection of new confirmed cases and deaths across multiple affected areas, alongside further expansion of the outbreak's geographic footprint. The epidemiological situation outside the Democratic Republic of the Congo remained stable, with no new cases or evidence of secondary transmission reported in Uganda or France. However, the persistence and geographic expansion of transmission within the Democratic Republic of the Congo continue to increase the risk of cross-border spread, highlighting the need for sustained regional surveillance, preparedness, and response efforts. Democratic Republic of the Congo Since the last update of 12 July 2026 (Situation Report #9), the epidemiological situation in the Democratic Republic of the Congo has continued to deteriorate, with sustained transmission and ongoing geographic expansion. An additional 460 confirmed cases and 248 confirmed deaths have been reported, representing increases of 23.4% and 34.5% in cumulative confirmed cases and confirmed deaths, respectively. The crude case fatality ratio (CFR) among confirmed cases increased from 36.6% to 39.9%, likely reflecting delayed case detection, late presentation for care, and the persistently high proportion of deaths occurring outside designated treatment facilities, rather than increased disease severity. During the reporting period, the outbreak expanded to five additional health zones across Haut-Uélé and Ituri provinces, increasing the total number of affected health zones from 42 to 47. The newly affected health zones were Pawa, Boma Mangbetu, and Isiro in Haut-Uélé Province, and Mahagi and Adja in Ituri Province. Despite this continued geographic expansion, recent transmission remains concentrated in a subset of affected areas. Of the 47 affected health zones, 40 have reported at least one confirmed case during the past 21 days. During this period, 1,090 confirmed cases and 568 confirmed deaths were reported. Ituri Province continues to bear the overwhelming burden of the outbreak, accounting for 946 cases (86.8%) and 476 deaths (83.8%), while the remaining four affected provinces together accounted for 144 cases (13.2%) and 92 deaths (16.2%). In contrast, seven affected health zones have now gone more than 21 consecutive days without reporting a confirmed case, suggesting an absence of recent transmission, provided that surveillance remains sufficiently sensitive to detect ongoing transmission. These health zones include Miti-Murhesa (60 days) in South Kivu Province; Aungba (30 days) and Gety (59 days) in Ituri Province; and Goma (55 days), Kalunguta (56 days), Mabalako (36 days), and Vuhovi (38 days) in North Kivu Province. Recent transmission remained highly concentrated in a limited number of health zones. The largest increases over the 21-day period were recorded in Bunia (281 cases), Nizi (173), Rwampara (155), Mongbwalu (105), Katwa (59), Lita (49), Nia-Nia (40), Mangala (37), and Bambu and Butembo (24 each). Together, these ten health zones accounted for approximately 86.9% of all additional confirmed cases reported during the period. Bunia, Rwampara, Mongbwalu, and Nizi remained the principal transmission corridor. The rapid increase in cases in Nizi, a health zone hosting several internally displaced persons (IDP) camps, together with the emergence of cases in Adja Health Zone, indicates continued westward and northward expansion of transmission within Ituri Province. Mortality was similarly concentrated. The largest increases in confirmed deaths over the same 21-day period were reported in Bunia (129 deaths), Nizi (84), Mongbwalu (80), Rwampara (73), Katwa (45), Mangala (26), Lita (18), Butembo (16), Nyankunde (13), and Beni (11). Collectively, these ten health zones accounted for approximately 87.0% of all additional confirmed deaths reported during the period. Overall, the distribution of confirmed deaths remained heavily skewed towards deaths occurring outside designated treatment centres, highlighting persistent delays in case detection, referral, and access to treatment. Between 24 June and 19 July 2026, 673 confirmed deaths were reported, of which 400 (59.4%) occurred outside designated treatment centres and 273 (40.6%) occurred within designated treatment centres. During the most recent reporting week (13 – 19 July 2026), mortality remained high, with community deaths (defined as deaths occurring outside designated treatment centres) accounting for 65.4% of all reported deaths, compared with 34.6% occurring within designated treatment centres. This persistent predominance of community deaths suggests that delays in case detection, referral, and timely access to treatment continue to contribute substantially to mortality and underscore the need to strengthen community surveillance, rapid referral, and early access to care. Since the beginning of the outbreak, the Democratic Republic of the Congo has reported 2,423 confirmed cases, including 967 confirmed deaths, corresponding to a crude case fatality ratio (CFR) of 39.9%. Ituri Province remains the epicentre, accounting for 2,160 confirmed cases (89.1%) and 811 confirmed deaths (83.9%) reported nationally. The most affected health zones continue to be Bunia (639 cases, 211 deaths), Rwampara (450 cases, 133 deaths), Mongbwalu (363 cases, 203 deaths), Nizi (214 cases, 93 deaths), Nyankunde (99 cases, 28 deaths), Lita (78 cases, 27 deaths), and Mangala (61 cases, 38 deaths) in Ituri Province, together with Katwa (104 cases, 72 deaths), Butembo (58 cases, 30 deaths), and Beni (37 cases, 25 deaths) in North Kivu Province. Collectively, these ten health zones account for approximately 86.8% of all confirmed cases (2,103 of 2,423) and 88.9% of all confirmed deaths (860 of 967) reported nationally, demonstrating that, despite continued geographic expansion, the burden of the outbreak remains highly concentrated. As of 19 July 2026, a total of 10,519 contacts were under follow-up in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, of whom 8,531 (81.1%) were successfully seen within the previous 24 hours. Ituri Province accounted for the majority of contacts under follow-up, with 7,537 contacts, including 6,123 (81.3%) successfully seen during the reporting period. In North Kivu Province, 1,801 of 2,149 contacts (83.8%) were successfully followed up, while Haut-Uélé Province, one of the newly affected provinces, reported 607 contacts, of whom only 317 (52.3%) were seen within the previous 24 hours. South Kivu Province had no contacts under active follow-up, reflecting the absence of recent transmission requiring contact monitoring, whereas contact follow-up data for Tshopo Province were not reported. The overall contact follow-up rate remains below the operational target for effective contact tracing. Although follow-up performance in Ituri Province improved slightly compared with previous weeks, more than 1,400 contacts were not reached during the reporting period. The markedly low follow-up rate in Haut-Uélé Province is of particular concern given the recent geographic expansion of the outbreak. These gaps increase the risk of undetected infections, missed chains of transmission, and sustained community transmission in both established and newly affected areas. Uganda No new confirmed cases have been reported in Uganda since the previous update. The most recent confirmed case, reported on 21 June 2026, was identified in a truck driver travelling along the Democratic Republic of the Congo– Uganda international route. Since then, no additional imported or locally acquired cases have been detected, and there is no evidence of ongoing transmission. As of 19 July 2026, the outbreak remains limited to 21 cases (20 confirmed and one probable), including three deaths (two confirmed and one probable). All 18 recovered patients have now been discharged from care, with the last confirmed patient discharged on 16 July 2026. Since the beginning of the outbreak, 836 contacts have been identified, all of whom successfully completed the required 21-day follow-up period, during which six secondary cases were detected. No contacts are currently under follow-up, reflecting the absence of active transmission chains. Following the discharge of the last confirmed patient on 16 July 2026, Uganda entered the 42-day countdown required to declare the end of the outbreak. As of 19 July 2026, the country was on Day 5 of the countdown. Nevertheless, Uganda remains at high risk of reintroduction due to the ongoing outbreak in the neighbouring Democratic Republic of the Congo. Continued population movement across the shared border underscores the importance of maintaining cross-border coordination, surveillance, rapid case detection, and response readiness until transmission has been interrupted in both countries. Location and population
Democratic Republic of the Congo, specifically in Ituri Province (epicenter), North Kivu Province, South Kivu Province, and Haut-Uélé Province. Affected population includes residents of 47 health zones, with notable transmission in Bunia, Nizi, Rwampara, Mongbwalu, and Katwa. Internally displaced persons (IDP) camps in Nizi are highlighted as a vulnerable group.
Event
Confirmed outbreak of Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD) in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, with sustained intense transmission, geographic expansion, and increased case and death counts. The situation is deteriorating, with 460 new confirmed cases and 248 new confirmed deaths reported since the last update. No new cases or secondary transmission reported in Uganda or France.
Scale, impact and risk
Cumulative confirmed cases: 2,423; confirmed deaths: 967; crude case fatality ratio (CFR): 39.9%. Ituri Province accounts for 89.1% of cases and 83.9% of deaths. Transmission is highly concentrated, with 10 health zones accounting for 86.8% of cases and 88.9% of deaths. Risk of cross-border spread remains high due to geographic expansion and persistence of transmission.
Response, status and uncertainty
Investigation and response efforts are ongoing, with challenges including delayed case detection, late presentation for care, and high proportion of deaths outside designated treatment centers (59.4% overall, 65.4% in the most recent week). Seven health zones have gone more than 21 days without confirmed cases, suggesting possible absence of recent transmission if surveillance is sensitive. Regional surveillance, preparedness, and response efforts are emphasized to mitigate cross-border spread.

我们对 埃博拉病毒 的 Bundibugyo 菌株了解多少? - DD新闻

What do we know about the Bundibugyo strain of the Ebola virus? - DD News
新闻简报由于罕见的本迪布焦 埃博拉病毒 暴发 影响刚果民主共和国和乌干达,世界卫生组织已宣布国际关注的突发公共卫生事件。该菌株已造成 100 多例疑似死亡和近 400 例感染,与致命性更强的扎伊尔菌株(高达 90%)相比,死亡率较低(30-40%)。传播 通过直接接触体液发生,给医护人员带来风险。尽管默克公司的 Ervebo 和来自中国的 mRNA 疫苗 等实验性候选药物显示出前景,但尚未获得批准的 治疗 或 疫苗 可用。应对工作的重点是公共卫生措施,包括快速检测和隔离,尽管早期检测的挑战延迟了初步干预措施。

时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 27 日;赛事详情包括在刚果民主共和国举办的 2007-2008 年 暴发 和 2012 年 暴发。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国和乌干达;受影响人群包括乌干达本迪布焦省的医院工作人员和个人。
事件
罕见的本迪布焦 埃博拉病毒 暴发 已被世界卫生组织宣布为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件;已有确诊病例和死亡病例报告。
规模、影响与风险
截至周一,已有超过100人疑似死亡,近400人疑似感染;本迪布焦病毒株的死亡率为 30-40%,低于扎伊尔病毒株高达 90% 的死亡率;传播 通过直接接触体液发生。
应对、状态与不确定性
不存在经批准的 疫苗 或 Bundibugyo 药物;应对措施依赖于病例检测、隔离和接触者追踪等公共卫生措施;早期测试最初未能发现这种菌株,从而延误了应对工作。
A high priorityPublic-health news

What do we know about the Bundibugyo strain of the Ebola virus? - DD News

News Brief
The World Health Organization has declared a public health emergency of international concern due to a rare Bundibugyo Ebola virus outbreak affecting the Democratic Republic of Congo and Uganda. With over 100 suspected deaths and nearly 400 infections, this strain has a lower fatality rate (30-40%) compared to the more lethal Zaire strain (up to 90%). Transmission occurs through direct contact with bodily fluids, posing risks to healthcare workers. No approved treatments or vaccines are available, though experimental candidates like Merck's Ervebo and an mRNA vaccine from China show promise. Response efforts focus on public health measures, including rapid detection and isolation, though early testing challenges delayed initial interventions.
Original source text (English)
Feedback | Tuesday, July 28, 2026 Skip Navigation DD News Menu Search Live Radio HI Top Stories National International Mann ki Baat Education Health Business & Economy Science & Tech Sports Commonwealth Environment Opinion Your browser does not support Javascript Tweets by DDNewslive Your browser does not support Javascript HomeTop StoriesWhat do we know about the Bundibugyo strain of the Ebola virus? 19/05/26 | 9:31 am | Ebola | World Health Organization Share Your browser does not support JavaScript What do we know about the Bundibugyo strain of the Ebola virus? File photo/getty images A rare strain of Ebola has prompted the World Health Organization to declare a public health emergency of international concern. Most of the cases have occurred in the Democratic Republic of Congo, with more than 100 suspected deaths and nearly 400 suspected infections as of Monday. Here is what we know about this strain of the Ebola virus, known as Bundibugyo. WHAT IS BUNDIBUGYO EBOLA? The current Ebola outbreak – so far limited to the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda – is due to a rare strain of the virus known as Bundibugyo, named after Uganda’s Bundibugyo province where it was first identified during an outbreak in 2007-2008. A second Bundibugyo outbreak occurred in 2012 in the DRC. Bundibugyo kills 30% to 40% of infected people, making it less lethal than the more common Zaire strain, which causes death in up to 90%, according to a global study published in 2024. Bundibugyo is one of the four species of ebolavirus genus that cause life-threatening illness in humans. All Ebola viruses are transmitted through direct contact with the bodily fluids of infected animals or humans or objects contaminated with such fluids. Body fluid transmission is a particular risk for hospital workers. A U.S. doctor working in the DRC has been infected in the current outbreak. According to the World Health Organization, ebola viruses initially cause flu-like symptoms including fever, fatigue, malaise, muscle pain, headache and sore throat that can start suddenly, followed by vomiting and diarrhea and eventually by internal and external bleeding and multi-organ failure. ARE THERE TREATMENTS FOR BUNDIBUGYO? There are no approved vaccines or drugs for Bundibugyo ebolavirus. Emergency use authorization would be necessary for deployment of any experimental treatments or existing treatments that have been effective against other strains. Potential candidates that have helped to control Bundibugyo in trials in non-human primates include Merck’s Ervebo, Mapp Biopharmaceutical’s MBP 134, and Auro Vaccines’ VesiculoVax. Nano Viricides said its experimental antiviral drug NV-387, currently in clinical trials against mpox, could be effective against the Bundibugyo strain. It mimics the immune cell surface proteins to which all ebolaviruses attach themselves and could thereby act as a decoy to “soak up” the virus and prevent it from attaching to healthy cells. Earlier in the pipeline, an mRNA vaccine being developed in China has shown promise against Bundibugyo in mice but has not yet been tested in primates. For now, response efforts will rely on public health measures such as rapid case detection, isolation, contact tracing, infection prevention and control, safe burials, and community engagement, said Dr. Daniela Manno of the London School of Hygiene & Tropical Medicine in a statement. “These measures were critical in eventually controlling the 2014–2016 West Africa Ebola epidemic, the largest Ebola outbreak ever recorded, and if implemented rapidly and effectively they can also help control this outbreak,” Manno said. IS THERE A TEST FOR BUNDIBUGYO? Tests for Bundibugyo exist but are not widely used. Initial analysis of samples in the current outbreak using standard tests did not detect the infections. “Because early tests looked for the wrong strain of Ebola, we got false negatives and lost weeks of response time,” Dr. Matthew Kavanagh, director of the Georgetown University Center for Global Health Policy & Politics in Washington, D.C., said in a statement. “By the time the alarm was raised, the virus had already moved along major transport routes and crossed borders,” Kavanagh said. WHAT MAKES BUNDIBUGYO DIFFERENT FROM OTHER STRAINS? Differences in genetic makeup between Bundibugyo and other ebolaviruses impact its virulence, or infectiousness, its diagnosis, and the availability of medical treatments. Compared to the Zaire strain, which quickly reproduces itself to reach high levels in the patient’s body, Bundibugyo replicates more slowly. Bundibugyo is also slower to invade, disable and kill immune cells, eventually crippling the patient’s immune defenses. The incubation periods for the Bundibugyo virus and the Zaire virus are nearly identical, however, averaging 8 to 10 days but sometimes lasting up to three weeks. A recent study of survivors of the 2007 Bundibugyo outbreak that found persistent symptoms and immune and metabolic alterations nevertheless concluded that overall, Bundibugyo may have less severe long-term effects on the liver and kidneys than the Zaire strain. 多国 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 情况报告第 10 号 - ReliefWeb

Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak Situation Report No. 10 - ReliefWeb
新闻简报As of 15 July 2026, the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) reported 2,124 confirmed cases of Ebola virus disease caused by the Bundibugyo virus, including 828 deaths. Uganda, which has not reported new cases since 21 June, has initiated a 42-day enhanced surveillance period to potentially declare the outbreak's end. The outbreak response includes extensive health screenings, with 8.6 million conducted at entry and control points, and 6.6 million people reached through risk communication activities. The International Organization for Migration (IOM) is supporting 215 points of entry and control points, reinforcing surveillance and cross-border preparedness. Despite progress, the outbreak persists in a complex environment marked by insecurity and population mobility, necessitating sustained efforts to prevent resurgence and cross-border transmission.

时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 24 日。活动日期:截至 2026 年 7 月 15 日。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国(DRC)和乌干达。受影响人口:未详细说明,但包括刚果民主共和国和乌干达的社区。
事件
刚果民主共和国确诊由本迪布焦病毒(BVD)引起的暴发,截至2026年7月15日已有2,124例确诊病例和828例死亡。乌干达自6月21日以来没有报告新病例,并开始为期42天的加强监测期。
规模、影响与风险
DRC: 2,124 confirmed cases, 828 deaths. Uganda: No new cases since 21 June. Risk: Cross-border transmission risk persists due to ongoing transmission in DRC. 8.6 million health screenings conducted at points of entry and control points. 6.6 million people reached through risk communication activities.
应对、状态与不确定性
监测 和准备措施正在进行中。乌干达处于 42 天增强型 监测 期间。IOM支持215个入境点和控制点,加强监测和筛查。《暴发》在充满不安全、流离失所和人口流动的复杂作战环境中展开。对 监测、边境卫生措施和社区参与的持续投资对于遏制 暴发 和防止跨境传播至关重要。

Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA DR Congo Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak Situation Report No. 10 Format Situation Report Source IOM Posted 24 Jul 2026 Originally published 24 Jul 2026 Attachments Download Report (PDF | 985.18 KB) According to the World Health Organization, as of 15 July, the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) reported 2,124 confirmed cases of Ebola disease caused by the Bundibugyo virus (BVD), including 828 deaths. In Uganda, no new cases had been reported since 21 June, and the country began the 42-day period of enhanced disease surveillance required before the end of the outbreak can be declared, following the discharge of the most recent case from treatment after two negative tests. Continued surveillance and preparedness measures remain essential to prevent resurgence and mitigate the risk of cross-border transmission while transmission persists in neighboring DRC. key highlights include : In total 8.6 million health screenings have been conducted at points of entry and points of control across affected and at-risk countries since the start of the response. During the reporting period alone, IOM supported more than 1.68 million screenings in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, generating 74 alerts and facilitating the verification and referral of suspected cases. 6.6 million people have been reached through risk communication and community engagement activities, helping communities access timely information and reduce transmission risks. IOM is currently supporting 215 points of entry and points of control across the region, strengthening surveillance, screening, infection prevention and cross-border preparedness efforts. Preparedness and response activities continued in priority countries, including DRC, Uganda, Burundi, Rwanda and South Sudan, while readiness measures were reinforced across other at-risk countries in the region. IOM’s mobility tracking and population mobility mapping work continues to support governments in identifying high-risk mobility corridors and strengthening cross-border surveillance and coordination The outbreak continues to unfold in a complex operational environment marked by insecurity, displacement and population mobility. As transmission persists in eastern DRC, sustained investment in surveillance, border health measures, community engagement, and support to displaced and mobile populations remains critical to containing the outbreak and preventing cross-border spread. 埃博拉 暴发:现状 - 疾病控制与预防中心 |疾病预防控制中心 (.gov)

Ebola Outbreak: Current Situation - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报CDC 正在应对由本迪布焦病毒在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 和乌干达迅速传播的埃博拉病毒 暴发,这是刚果民主共和国出现的第 17 例埃博拉病毒 暴发。截至2026年7月下旬,暴发已导致3,283例确诊病例和1,439人死亡,成为有记录以来第三大埃博拉暴发。暴发 的传播速度比之前的更快,40 天内就超过了 1000 例。虽然美国尚未确诊病例,但疾病预防控制中心已采取公共卫生措施,包括入境筛查和旅行限制,以防止疾病进入该国。应对措施面临医疗基础设施有限、持续冲突和人口跨境流动等挑战。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 24 日。活动日期:暴发 截至 2026 年 7 月 28 日持续进行。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国(DRC)和乌干达。刚果民主共和国受影响省份:上韦莱省、伊图里省、北基伍省、南基伍省、乔波省。乌干达坎帕拉报告病例。
事件
CDC 正在应对刚果民主共和国和乌干达偏远地区由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒 暴发。这是刚果民主共和国第 17 个埃博拉病毒 暴发,其传播速度比之前的 暴发 更快。
规模、影响与风险
截至 2026 年 7 月 27 日,确诊病例总数:3,283 例(刚果民主共和国:3,262 例,乌干达:20 例,法国:1 例)。确诊死亡总数:1,439 人(刚果民主共和国:1,437 人,乌干达:2 人,法国:0 人)。可能病例:1(乌干达)。暴发 是有记录以来的第三大病例,在 40 天内就超过了 1,000 例。美国公众和旅行者面临的风险仍然很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC、DHS 和其他联邦机构于 2026 年 5 月 18 日实施了公共卫生入境筛查和限制。2026 年 7 月 13 日发布了继续暂停特定外国人入境的命令,为期 30 天。疾病预防控制中心建议避免前往刚果民主共和国上韦莱省、伊图里省、北基伍省和乔波省的非必要旅行。挑战包括有限的卫生基础设施、持续的冲突、针对医护人员的暴力、人口流动和错误信息。其他案件的调查正在进行中。

Ebola Outbreak: Current Situation - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
The CDC is responding to a rapidly spreading Ebola outbreak caused by Bundibugyo virus in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda, marking the 17th Ebola outbreak in DRC. As of late July 2026, the outbreak has resulted in 3,283 confirmed cases and 1,439 deaths, making it the third largest Ebola outbreak on record. The outbreak has spread faster than previous ones, surpassing 1,000 cases within 40 days. While no cases have been confirmed in the United States, the CDC has implemented public health measures, including entry screening and travel restrictions, to prevent the disease from entering the country. The response faces challenges such as limited healthcare infrastructure, ongoing conflict, and population movement across borders.

Ebola Explore This Topic Search Search Clear Search For Everyone Current Situation About Signs and Symptoms Outbreak History How It Spreads FAQs Ebola Communication Resources View all Health Care Providers Clinical Guidance Clinical Features Vaccine Product Information Non-U.S. Settings Toolkit Training View all Public Health Interim Public Health Travel Guidance Public Health Strategy View all search clear search Ebola Menu clear search For Everyone Current Situation About Signs and Symptoms Outbreak History How It Spreads FAQs Ebola Communication Resources View All Home Health Care Providers Clinical Guidance Clinical Features Vaccine Product Information Non-U.S. Settings Toolkit Training View All Home Public Health Interim Public Health Travel Guidance Public Health Strategy View All Home Related Topics About Viral Hemorrhagic Fevers Current Situation About Signs and Symptoms Outbreak History How It Spreads FAQs Ebola Communication Resources View Menu Viral Hemorrhagic Fevers Ebola Outbreak: Current Situation For Everyone July 28, 2026 Español Key points CDC is responding to an outbreak of Ebola disease caused by Bundibugyo virus in remote areas of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda. To date, no cases of Ebola disease have been confirmed in the United States because of this outbreak. The overall risk to the American public and travelers remains low. Outbreak overview Risk to the United States To date, no Ebola cases associated with this outbreak have been reported in the United States. The likelihood of Ebola spreading to the United States is considered very low.If a case was diagnosed in the United States, the risk of Ebola spreading in the United States is also low due to the strength of our public health system and infection control measures. CDC is responding to an outbreak of Ebola in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda. The outbreak is caused by Bundibugyo virus, a type of Ebola virus, in northeastern DRC and Uganda's capital Kampala. In DRC, cases have been confirmed in Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu (North Kivu), Sud-Kivu (South Kivu), and Tshopo provinces. This is the 17th outbreak of Ebola in DRC. This outbreak is spreading substantially faster than previous Ebola outbreaks and is now the third largest Ebola outbreak on record. This Ebola outbreak surpassed 1,000 confirmed cases within 40 days of response activation. In comparison, the 2018 Ebola outbreak in North Kivu, DRC, took approximately 235 days to reach more than 1,000 cases. South Kivu has reported no confirmed cases since May 29. The number of reported deaths has risen rapidly as well, with more than 100 fatalities reported in the first week of July. All cases in Uganda so far have been diagnosed in its capital of Kampala, with no community spread reported. Uganda's last reported case, confirmed on June 21, was related to travel from DRC. Case counts during the first 100 days of the 2026 Ebola outbreak versus the same period in outbreaks in 2014 and 2018 Case counts during the first 100 days of the 2026 Ebola outbreak versus the same period in outbreaks in 2014 and 2018Show Text Description The response to this outbreak is challenging due to a number of complex circumstances in the region. Limited health infrastructure, including limited access to healthcare, diagnostic and laboratory services, infection prevention and control supplies, and other basic services make stopping this outbreak more difficult. Ongoing conflict in the area where the outbreak is occurring creates security issues and complicates contact tracing and infection prevention and control. Violence against healthcare workers and shortages of personal protective equipment are leading to healthcare workers becoming infected while treating patients. Frequent population movement and cross-border travel increase the risk of spread to Uganda, South Sudan, Rwanda, and to other areas. Lack of trust in the government and misinformation make communicating about Ebola more difficult. CDC is assessing travelers arriving in the United States who have recently been in DRC or Uganda, as well as neighboring South Sudan, for symptoms of and possible exposure to Ebola. On May 18, 2026, CDC, the Department of Homeland Security, and other appropriate federal agencies implemented public health entry screening, entry restrictions, and other public health measures to prevent Ebola disease from entering the United States. An Order continuing the suspension of the right to introduce specified foreign nationals into the United States was issued on July 13, 2026. It will be in effect for 30 days. CDC recommends avoiding non-essential travel to Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu (North Kivu), and Tshopo provinces in DRC. Travelers to other parts of DRC or Uganda should take precautions to avoid Ebola exposure and monitor for symptoms while traveling and for 21 days after leaving. Latest data Reported cases This is a rapidly evolving situation, and case counts are subject to change. The Uganda MoH does not include suspected cases in their official reporting.Map of affected areas View LargerDownload To date, the Ebola disease outbreak in DRC has been confirmed in Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu (North Kivu), Sud-Kivu (South Kivu), and Tshopo provinces. Cases related to the DRC outbreak also have been reported in Uganda's capital of Kampala. CDC response CDC is working internationally and domestically to respond to this outbreak and prevent Ebola from entering the United States. About 400 people at CDC are involved in responding to the outbreak, including more than 120 people deployed to the affected countries. View five news elements
Time
Publication date: July 24, 2026. Event date: Outbreak ongoing as of July 28, 2026.
Location and population
Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda. Affected provinces in DRC: Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu, Sud-Kivu, Tshopo. Uganda cases reported in Kampala.
Event
CDC is responding to an outbreak of Ebola disease caused by Bundibugyo virus in remote areas of DRC and Uganda. This is the 17th outbreak of Ebola in DRC and is spreading faster than previous outbreaks.
Scale, impact and risk
As of July 27, 2026, total confirmed cases: 3,283 (DRC: 3,262, Uganda: 20, France: 1). Total confirmed deaths: 1,439 (DRC: 1,437, Uganda: 2, France: 0). Probable cases: 1 (Uganda). The outbreak is the third largest on record and surpassed 1,000 cases within 40 days. Risk to the American public and travelers remains low.
Response, status and uncertainty
CDC, DHS, and other federal agencies implemented public health entry screening and restrictions on May 18, 2026. An Order continuing suspension of entry for specified foreign nationals was issued on July 13, 2026, for 30 days. CDC recommends avoiding non-essential travel to Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu, and Tshopo provinces in DRC. Challenges include limited health infrastructure, ongoing conflict, violence against healthcare workers, population movement, and misinformation. Investigation of additional cases is ongoing.

为从埃博拉病毒感染地区返回的旅客提供的信息 - 疾病控制和预防中心 |疾病预防控制中心 (.gov)

Information for Travelers Returning from Ebola-Affected Areas - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报继刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 和乌干达实施 暴发 后,疾病预防控制中心正在实施公共卫生措施,以防止埃博拉病毒输入美国。21天内去过刚果民主共和国的旅客被限制进入美国,而来自乌干达或南苏丹的旅客必须在指定机场接受加强检查。建议所有旅客在接触埃博拉病毒后 21 天内监测埃博拉症状。这些措施于 2026 年 5 月 18 日宣布,并于 2026 年 7 月 13 日延长 30 天,反映了迅速变化的 暴发 局势。

发布于2026年7月23日。活动措施于2026年5月18日宣布,并于2026年7月13日延长30天。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国 (DRC)、乌干达和南苏丹。受影响人群:从这些国家返回美国的旅行者。
事件
CDC 正在应对刚果民主共和国和乌干达的埃博拉疾病 暴发。入境限制和加强筛查措施已到位,以防止埃博拉病毒输入。
规模、影响与风险
没有提供具体病例数。通过旅行限制、入境筛查和对旅行者进行 21 天症状监测,输入风险正在降低。
应对、状态与不确定性
措施包括入境限制、指定机场筛查、症状监测和公共卫生后续行动。暴发 范围正在迅速发展,措施可能会扩展。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Information for Travelers Returning from Ebola-Affected Areas - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
The CDC is implementing public health measures to prevent Ebola importation into the United States following an outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda. Travelers who have been in DRC within 21 days are restricted from entering the U.S., while those from Uganda or South Sudan must undergo enhanced screening at designated airports. All travelers are advised to monitor for Ebola symptoms for 21 days post-exposure. The measures, announced on May 18, 2026, were extended on July 13, 2026, for 30 days, reflecting the rapidly evolving outbreak situation.
Original source text (English)

Ebola Explore This Topic Search Search Clear Search For Everyone Current Situation About Signs and Symptoms Outbreak History How It Spreads FAQs Ebola Communication Resources View all Health Care Providers Clinical Guidance Clinical Features Vaccine Product Information Non-U.S. Settings Toolkit Training View all Public Health Interim Public Health Travel Guidance Public Health Strategy View all search clear search Ebola Menu clear search For Everyone Current Situation About Signs and Symptoms Outbreak History How It Spreads FAQs Ebola Communication Resources View All Home Health Care Providers Clinical Guidance Clinical Features Vaccine Product Information Non-U.S. Settings Toolkit Training View All Home Public Health Interim Public Health Travel Guidance Public Health Strategy View All Home Related Topics About Viral Hemorrhagic Fevers Current Situation About Signs and Symptoms Outbreak History How It Spreads FAQs Ebola Communication Resources View Menu Viral Hemorrhagic Fevers Information for Travelers Returning from Ebola-Affected Areas For Everyone July 23, 2026 Español What to know CDC is responding to an outbreak of Ebola disease in areas of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda. The full scope of the outbreak is rapidly evolving. U.S. entry for all travelers who were recently in DRC and certain travelers who were recently in Uganda or South Sudan is temporarily restricted. Travelers should monitor themselves for Ebola symptoms for 21 days after leaving affected countries. Anyone who develops symptoms should avoid travel and contact public health authorities immediately. Background CDC is assessing travelers arriving in the United States who have recently been in DRC or Uganda, as well as neighboring South Sudan, for symptoms of and possible exposure to Ebola. To reduce the risk of Ebola importation into the United States, U.S. entry for travelers who were recently in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) is temporarily restricted. Travelers, including Americans, who have been in DRC within 21 days of their flight will not be allowed to board commercial flights with U.S. destinations. All U.S. citizens and U.S. nationals who have been in the DRC should plan to remain outside DRC for 21 days before entering the United States. U.S. citizens and U.S. nationals who have been present in Uganda or South Sudan (and not present in DRC) within 21 days of arrival in the United States must only enter through designated airports for enhanced screening. Please review the CDC's information on what travelers need to know about returning to the United States from DRC, Uganda, and South Sudan for the list of designated airports and for health information about Ebola. More information for travelers Americans who need consular assistance can visit Additional Consular Information for Americans Regarding the Ebola Outbreak or call the Department of State:+1-202-501-4444 (from abroad)+1-888-407-4747 (from the U.S. and Canada On May 18, 2026, CDC, DHS, and other appropriate federal agencies announced public health entry screening, entry restrictions, and other public health measures to prevent Ebola disease from entering the United States. An Order continuing the suspension of the right to introduce specified foreign nationals into the United States was issued on July 13, 2026. It will be in effect for 30 days. Keep Reading Order: Suspending the Right to Introduce Certain Persons from Countries Where a Quarantinable Communicable Disease Exists Before travel to the U.S. CDC is temporarily restricting U.S. entry for all travelers who were recently in DRC and certain travelers who were recently in Uganda or South Sudan. There are case-by-case humanitarian or law enforcement exceptions. For more information, see Ebola and Bundibugyo Virus Frequently Asked Questions. Air passengers who have been in Uganda and South Sudan in the past 21 days and are allowed to enter the United States will have their travel rerouted to a designated airport for public health entry screening. Airlines will work with affected travelers to rebook flights. These airports are: • Washington-Dulles International Airport (IAD) in Virginia outside Washington, D.C. • Hartsfield-Jackson Atlanta International Airport (ATL) in Atlanta, Georgia • George Bush Intercontinental Airport (IAH) in Houston, Texas • John F. Kennedy International Airport (JFK) in New York City If someone has only transited through Uganda or South Sudan, the Department of Homeland Security (DHS) will determine if they need to be redirected to a designated U.S. airport. Entry screening Travelers permitted to enter the United States who have recently been in South Sudan or Uganda will undergo public health entry screening. During entry screening, travelers may Be escorted to a designated screening area Complete a brief questionnaire about travel history and symptoms Have their temperature checked using a non-contact thermometer Be observed for signs of illness by CDC staff Be enrolled in automated text messages from CDC to remind them to monitor their health for 21 days Some travelers may have an additional public health assessment if they have been in certain situations. CDC may also share contact information with state or local public health authorities if needed for follow-up and support. Travelers without symptoms Travelers without symptoms will receive information about monitoring their health for 21 days after leaving the affected country. Most travelers without symptoms will continue to their final destination after public health entry screening. Travelers with symptoms during screening Travelers with fever or other symptoms that could be Ebola will receive additional evaluation by a CDC public health officer. If the assessment shows that a traveler may be sick with Ebola, the traveler will be transferred to a hospital for medical evaluation, isolation, and care. Steps to take after travel If you have been in DRC, South Sudan, or UgandaWatch your health for 21 days after you left the country. CDC will send you automated text messages reminding you to watch for symptoms and to take action if you get sick. If you were in DRC or UgandaTake your temperature every day and again if you feel sick. If you were in South SudanTake your temperature if you feel sick. Watch for symptomsWatch for Ebola symptoms such as fever (100.4°F/38°C or higher) or feeling feverish, headache or body aches, rash, weakness or tiredness, sore throat, diarrhea, vomiting, stomach pain, unexplained bleeding or bruising (a late stage of illness). Follow instructions from public health authorities Your health department will follow up with you during the 21 days after you left the affected country. This follow-up is determined by which of the affected countries (Uganda vs. South Sudan) you were in during the previous 21 days and what you did there. Public health authorities may provide additional instructions for some travelers. Keep in mind While you are being monitored in the United States, if you plan to travel domestically or internationally, tell your health department before you travel. Advance notification to public health authorities at your destination can also help prevent unforeseen disruptions to travel plans (e.g., denial of entry). CDC recommends that you remain in the United States and avoid international travel and cruise ship travel during your 21-day monitoring period. For more information visit Ebola and Bundibugyo Virus Frequently Asked Questions. What to do if you get sick If you develop signs or symptoms of Ebola disease: Separate yourself from others (isolate). Call your local health department or the health authorities where you are for advice about medical care. Tell the health department about your recent travel from a country with risk of Ebola and what your symptoms are so they can identify the best healthcare facility for you to go to, if needed. The health department can help the facility prepare to provide care for you and take any precautions needed. Follow all their instructions. Do not travel while sick. Travel only when you have been cleared by a doctor or health official. If you can't reach your health department right away and you need urgent medical care Call a healthcare provider. Tell them your symptoms and that you were recently in a country with risk of Ebola. Calling ahead before going to a healthcare facility helps the facility prepare for your arrival, including contacting the health department and taking any precautions needed. Resources Questions? Ebola Outbreak Surpasses 3,000 Cases as First Human Vaccine Trial for Rare Bundibugyo Virus Begins - Discover Magazine
新闻简报《Discover》杂志报道称,埃博拉 暴发 病例已超过 3,000 例,标志着一个重大的公共卫生问题。与此同时,针对罕见本迪布焦病毒的首次人体 疫苗 试验已经开始,为对抗这种特定的埃博拉病毒变种提供了潜在进展。虽然 暴发 的规模很大,但所提供的证据中尚未披露受影响地区、暴发 比率和具体应对措施的详细信息。疫苗 试验代表了解决 Bundibugyo 病毒问题的关键一步,该病毒是 埃博拉病毒 家族的一部分,但与更常见的已知毒株不同。该报告强调了打击 埃博拉病毒 的持续挑战以及有针对性的研究工作的重要性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Ebola Outbreak Surpasses 3,000 Cases as First Human Vaccine Trial for Rare Bundibugyo Virus Begins - Discover Magazine

News Brief
Discover Magazine reports that an Ebola outbreak has surpassed 3,000 cases, marking a significant public health concern. Concurrently, the first human vaccine trial for the rare Bundibugyo virus has commenced, offering potential progress against this specific Ebola variant. While the outbreak's scale is substantial, details on affected regions, mortality rates, and specific response measures remain undisclosed in the provided evidence. The vaccine trial represents a critical step in addressing Bundibugyo virus, which is part of the Ebola virus family but distinct from more commonly known strains. The report underscores the ongoing challenges in combating Ebola viruses and the importance of targeted research efforts.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

第一位志愿者在世界上首次本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 疫苗 试验中接种疫苗 - 牛津大学

First volunteer vaccinated in the world's first Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine trial - University of Oxford
新闻简报牛津大学宣布,世界上第一个本迪布焦埃博拉病毒(BDBV)疫苗临床试验的第一位志愿者取得了成功。这一里程碑由牛津 疫苗 小组领导,标志着针对 疫苗 物种之一开发保护措施的关键努力的开始。尽管没有报告 暴发 或直接风险,但该试验代表了全球针对 埃博拉病毒 的卫生准备工作取得了重大进展。该公告于 2026 年 7 月 24 日发布,强调了为应对新出现的传染病威胁而正在进行的研究工作。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 24 日。未指定事件日期,但据报道发生在发布当天。
地点与对象
英国牛津大学。该试验涉及志愿者,但证据中没有报告具体人群的详细信息。
事件
牛津大学牛津 疫苗 小组在世界上首次 疫苗 临床试验中成功为第一位志愿者接种了疫苗,该试验旨在预防本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 (BDBV)。这是已确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
没有病例数、死亡或暴露报告。该事件标志着本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 疫苗 的首次临床试验的启动,代表了全球卫生准备方面的一个重要里程碑,但并未描述 暴发 或当前风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
试验正在进行中,第一名志愿者已接种疫苗。未提供有关调查状态、测试、隔离、接触者追踪或 治疗 的信息。消息来源为大学官方公告,证据仅限于审判启动时。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

First volunteer vaccinated in the world's first Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine trial - University of Oxford

News Brief
The University of Oxford has announced the successful vaccination of the first volunteer in the world's first clinical trial for a Bundibugyo ebolavirus (BDBV) vaccine. Conducted by the Oxford Vaccine Group, this milestone marks the beginning of a critical effort to develop protection against one of the Ebola virus species. The trial represents a significant advancement in global health preparedness against Ebola viruses, though no outbreak or immediate risk is reported. The announcement, made on 24 July 2026, underscores ongoing research efforts to address emerging infectious disease threats.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

研究表明,可用的埃博拉 疫苗 可以预防本迪布焦病毒 - MedPage Today

Available Ebola Vaccine May Protect Against Bundibugyo Virus, Study Suggests - MedPage Today
新闻简报《新英格兰医学杂志》上发表的一项新研究表明,现有的埃博拉 疫苗 可能对本迪布焦病毒提供部分保护,该病毒正在刚果和乌干达造成持续的 暴发。研究人员在接种扎伊尔 埃博拉病毒 疫苗 疫苗的个体中检测到交叉反应性抗体反应,尽管这些反应比针对原始病毒的反应要弱。该研究支持评估单剂量 rVSVΔG-ZEBOV-GP 疫苗,该药物在当前的 暴发 期间可在全球库存中获得。However,the WHO has cautioned against its use outside of trials due to insufficient evidence.截至 2026 年 7 月下旬,暴发 已导致 2,557 例确诊病例和 1,035 例死亡,潜在疗法的临床试验正在进行中。
查看新闻五要素

发布日期:2026 年 7 月 23 日。活动时间范围:截至 2026 年 7 月下旬正在进行 暴发。

刚果和邻国乌干达。Affected population: Individuals in the Bundibugyo virus 暴发,with 2,557 confirmed cases and 1,035 confirmed deaths as of late July 2026.
事件
一项研究表明,现有的埃博拉 疫苗 可以提供针对本迪布焦病毒的部分保护,该病毒正在刚果和乌干达推动 暴发 的流行。在接种疫苗的个体中检测到交叉反应性抗体反应,但反应低于扎伊尔 埃博拉病毒。该研究支持评估当前 暴发 期间可用的 疫苗。
规模、影响与风险
确诊病例:2,557 例。确认死亡人数:1,035 人。实际数字可能更大。对于本迪布焦病毒,尚无获得许可的 疫苗 或疗法。该研究检测到交叉反应抗体反应,但未证实临床保护作用。
应对、状态与不确定性
临床试验正在进行中,以测试本迪布焦病毒的潜在疗法。世界卫生组织表示,没有足够的证据支持在试验之外使用 rVSVΔG-ZEBOV-GP 疫苗 来对抗本迪布焦病毒。全球紧急储备包含约 50 万剂 疫苗,但其使用目前仅限于试验。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Available Ebola Vaccine May Protect Against Bundibugyo Virus, Study Suggests - MedPage Today

News Brief
A new study published in the New England Journal of Medicine suggests that available Ebola vaccines may provide partial protection against the Bundibugyo virus, which is causing an ongoing outbreak in Congo and Uganda. Researchers detected cross-reactive antibody responses in individuals vaccinated with Zaire Ebola virus vaccines, though the responses were weaker than against the original virus. The study supports evaluating the single-dose rVSVΔG-ZEBOV-GP vaccine, which is available in the global stockpile, during the current outbreak. However, the WHO has cautioned against its use outside of trials due to insufficient evidence. The outbreak has resulted in 2,557 confirmed cases and 1,035 deaths as of late July 2026, with clinical trials underway for potential therapies.
Original source text (English)

Infectious Disease > Ebola Available Ebola Vaccine May Protect Against Bundibugyo Virus, Study Suggests — Cross-reactive antibody responses were detected with vaccines for Zaire Ebola virus disease by Terrence Rudd, Staff Writer, MedPage Today July 23, 2026 • 3 min read Add MedPage Today on Google MedpageToday Key Takeaways There are no licensed vaccines against Bundibugyo virus, the filovirus driving the Ebola outbreak in Congo and Uganda.Using serum samples from children and adults who took part in a randomized trial of vaccines for Zaire Ebola virus disease, cross-reactive antibody responses against Bundibugyo virus were detected at 28 days and 3 months.The findings "support the evaluation of readily available Ebola vaccines during the current outbreak," researchers said. An available vaccine for Ebola might provide partial protection against the ongoing outbreak of Bundibugyo virus, which has no licensed vaccines or therapeutics, according to new research. Using 179 serum samples from children and adults who took part in a randomized trial of vaccines for Zaire Ebola virus disease, cross-reactive antibody responses against Bundibugyo virus were detected across vaccine formulations at 28 days and 3 months, though responses were lower, reported Yves Levy, MD, PhD, of Université Paris Est Créteil in France, and colleagues in a correspondence to the New England Journal of Medicineopens in a new tab or window. In the samples from people vaccinated with the only licensed Ebola vaccine, Merck's single-dose live-attenuated vaccine (rVSVΔG-ZEBOV-GP; Ervebo)opens in a new tab or window, the median fluorescence intensity -- a measure of immune response -- against Bundibugyo virus was 282 in samples taken 28 days after vaccination and 130 in samples taken 3 months after vaccination versus 1,788 and 1,069, respectively, against Kikwit Ebola. Similar findings were seen in the rVSVΔG-ZEBOV-GP booster group. After administration of the heterologous Ad26.ZEBOV/MVA-BN-Filo vaccine, the response was higher at 3 months than at 28 days (median 433 vs 83) but remained lower than the corresponding Kikwit Ebola responses. "In the absence of a [Bundibugyo virus]-specific vaccine, these data support the evaluation of readily available Ebola vaccines during the current outbreak," Levy and colleagues wrote. In particular, the single-dose rVSVΔG-ZEBOV-GP vaccine is available in the global Ebola vaccine stockpile and "represents an immediately deployable candidate that could be evaluated during the ongoing outbreak," they noted. Researchers in Lancet Infectious Diseasesopens in a new tab or window this week also supported testing rVSVΔG-ZEBOV-GP against the Bundibugyo virus. They found that among more than 1,000 cases in the current outbreak, nine individuals who had been vaccinated with the live-attenuated vaccine and later contracted Bundibugyo all survived. In addition, among 242 deaths where vaccination status was known, none had received the vaccine. The global emergency stockpile of rVSVΔG-ZEBOV-GP has about half a million doses, but the World Health Organization has said the vaccineopens in a new tab or window should be used exclusively in trials, saying there is still not enough evidence to support its use against the Bundibugyo virus. (The heterologous Ad26.ZEBOV/MVA-BN-Filo vaccine is no longer available after being withdrawn from the European market earlier this year for commercial reasons.) Medical News from Around the Web1Discover MagazineEbola Outbreak Surpasses 3,000 Cases as First Human Vaccine Trial for Rare Bundibugyo Virus Beginsopens in a new tab or window2CNNNew US guidelines may affect how young adults manage ‘bad’ cholesterol | CNNopens in a new tab or window3Clinical AdvisorVaginal Estrogen Reduces Risk of Serious Adverse Outcomes in Recurrent UTIopens in a new tab or window4TimeMeasles Cases in the U.S. Are at a 35-Year Highopens in a new tab or window5CBS NewsA grandfather struggled with treatment for late-stage pancreatic cancer — until a clinical trial helped him beat the oddsopens in a new tab or windowSince at least May, Bundibugyo virusopens in a new tab or window has fueled an Ebola outbreakopens in a new tab or window in Congo and neighboring Uganda that's caused 2,557 confirmed cases and 1,035 confirmed deaths as of late Julyopens in a new tab or window, though actual numbers are likely much greateropens in a new tab or window. Clinical trials are underwayopens in a new tab or window to test potential therapies for the Bundibugyo virus. Levy and colleagues analyzed serum samples taken from West African adults and children who had been enrolled in the randomized PREVAC trialopens in a new tab or window, which compared vaccination regimens using rVSVΔG-ZEBOV-GP (single-dose or prime booster) or Ad26.ZEBOV/MVA-BN-Filo. Of the 179 samples, 55 were collected 28 days after vaccination and 124 were collected at 3 months. It's not clear whether the binding antibodies generated by the existing Ebola vaccines would have a positive protective value against Bundibugyo virus, Levy and colleagues acknowledged, but "glycoprotein-binding antibody levels have previously been associated with vaccine-induced protection." Publication date: July 23, 2026. Event timeframe: Ongoing outbreak as of late July 2026.
Location and population
Congo and neighboring Uganda. Affected population: Individuals in the Bundibugyo virus outbreak, with 2,557 confirmed cases and 1,035 confirmed deaths as of late July 2026.
Event
A study suggests that available Ebola vaccines may provide partial protection against Bundibugyo virus, which is driving an ongoing outbreak in Congo and Uganda. Cross-reactive antibody responses were detected in vaccinated individuals, though responses were lower than against Zaire Ebola virus. The study supports evaluating available vaccines during the current outbreak.
Scale, impact and risk
Confirmed cases: 2,557. Confirmed deaths: 1,035. Actual numbers are likely greater. No licensed vaccines or therapeutics exist for Bundibugyo virus. The study detected cross-reactive antibody responses but did not confirm clinical protection.
Response, status and uncertainty
Clinical trials are underway to test potential therapies for Bundibugyo virus. The WHO has stated there is insufficient evidence to support the use of the rVSVΔG-ZEBOV-GP vaccine against Bundibugyo virus outside of trials. The global emergency stockpile contains about half a million doses of the vaccine, but its use is currently restricted to trials.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

现有的 疫苗 诱导针对驱动刚果 暴发 的埃博拉病毒的抗体反应

Existing vaccines induce antibody response against the Ebolavirus that's driving Congo outbreak
新闻简报最近发表在《新英格兰医学杂志》上的一项研究表明,现有的埃博拉 疫苗 可能对本迪布焦病毒有一定的保护作用,该病毒正在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 推动埃博拉 暴发 病毒增长最快,自 2 月份以来已夺去了 1,000 多人的生命。研究人员测试了 179 名西非成年人和儿童的血清样本,这些成年人和儿童接种了默克公司的 Ervebo 或强生公司的两剂疫苗,发现针对 Bundibugyo 的交叉反应免疫力,尽管比针对扎伊尔的交叉反应免疫力弱。虽然还没有明确的保护证据,但结果令人鼓舞,特别是 Bundibugyo 专用的 疫苗 仍处于早期试验阶段。包括 Boghuma Titanji 博士在内的专家认为,暴发 的严重性需要立即考虑从全球库存中部署可用的扎伊尔 疫苗,以平衡不确定性与紧急干预需求。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-07-23;事件调查结果发表在《新英格兰医学杂志》上。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国;疫苗 试验中的西非成人和儿童(179 名参与者)。
事件
《新英格兰医学杂志》上发表的研究表明,现有的埃博拉 疫苗(默克公司的 Ervebo 和强生公司的 Zabdeno/Mvabea)会诱导一些针对本迪布焦病毒的抗体反应,这助长了刚果有记录以来增长最快的埃博拉 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
Bundibugyo 暴发 是有记录以来第三大埃博拉病毒 暴发,自 2 月份以来在刚果民主共和国夺去了 1,000 多人的生命。针对 Bundibugyo 的抗体反应比针对扎伊尔的抗体反应弱(默克公司的 疫苗 低八倍,强生公司的 疫苗 低六倍)。
应对、状态与不确定性
牛津大学启动了一项针对本迪布焦特异性疫苗的1期试验。默克公司的疫苗可通过全球埃博拉病毒疫苗储备库获得,并可在正在进行的暴发期间进行评估。这些发现并非保护的确凿证据,但令人鼓舞,促使世界卫生组织考虑部署扎伊尔埃博拉病毒疫苗储备库,尽管完善证据的时间有限。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Existing vaccines induce antibody response against the Ebolavirus that's driving Congo outbreak

News Brief
A recent study published in the New England Journal of Medicine reveals that existing Ebola vaccines may offer some protection against the Bundibugyo virus, which is driving the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), claiming over 1,000 lives since February. Researchers tested serum samples from 179 West African adults and children vaccinated with either Merck's Ervebo or Johnson & Johnson's two-dose regimen, finding cross-reactive immunity against Bundibugyo, though weaker than against Zaire. While not definitive proof of protection, the results are encouraging, especially as a Bundibugyo-specific vaccine is still in early trials. Experts, including Dr. Boghuma Titanji, argue that the outbreak's severity warrants immediate consideration of deploying available Zaire vaccines from global stockpiles, balancing uncertainty against the urgent need for intervention.
Original source text (English)

The findings, published yesterday in the New England Journal of Medicine, offer hopeful news as Bundibugyo is fueling the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak on record, claiming more than 1,000 lives in the Democratic Republic of the Congo since February.Bundibugyo belongs to a group of viruses that cause Ebola disease. A phase 1 trial to assess a vaccine that specifically targets Bundibugyo was launched earlier this month by the University of Oxford, as currently there are only licensed vaccines for the Zaire Ebolavirus, the most common of the six known Ebola speciesTo assess whether the Zaire vaccines could be used in the current outbreak, researchers took serum samples from 179 West African adults and children vaccinated with one of two licensed vaccines to see whether there was any antibody response against Bundibugyo. While not as strong as for Zaire, the Bundibugyo samples showed some cross-reactive immunity.Specifically, three months after a single dose of Merck's Ervebo vaccine, the antibody response was about eight times lower for Bundibugyo compared with Zaire. At the same point, the two-dose Ebola vaccine regimen produced by a subsidiary of Johnson & Johnson—Zabdeno and Mvabea—elicited six times more antibodies for Zaire than for Bundibugyo.The authors note that their results are consistent with those in studies involving nonhuman primates, and that the Merck vaccine, which is currently available through the global Ebola vaccine stockpile, "represents an immediately deployable candidate that could be evaluated during the ongoing outbreak."Limited time for perfect evidenceThe findings are not proof that these vaccines provide protection against Bundibugyo, but are still encouraging, said Boghuma K. Titanji, MD, DTM&H, PhD.In a thread posted on the social media platform Bluesky, Titanji said that in the absence of a licensed vaccine, the data should prompt the World Health Organization (WHO) to revisit whether to deploy stockpiles of the Zaire Ebola vaccine. I do not think this outbreak affords us the luxury to wait much longer.At this point, the Bundibugyo outbreak is the third-largest Ebola outbreak on record.Titanji noted that rarely do public health officials and clinicians have "perfect evidence" when responding to an infectious disease outbreak. Decision makers must balance uncertainty against "the very real cost of waiting.""I do not think this outbreak affords us the luxury to wait much longer," she said. View five news elements
Time
Published on 2026-07-23; Event findings published in the New England Journal of Medicine.
Location and population
Democratic Republic of the Congo; West African adults and children (179 participants) in the vaccine trial.
Event
Research published in the New England Journal of Medicine shows existing Ebola vaccines (Merck's Ervebo and Johnson & Johnson's Zabdeno/Mvabea) induce some antibody response against the Bundibugyo virus, which is fueling the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak on record in the DRC.
Scale, impact and risk
The Bundibugyo outbreak is the third-largest Ebola outbreak on record, claiming more than 1,000 lives in the DRC since February. The antibody response against Bundibugyo was weaker than against Zaire (eight times lower for Merck's vaccine, six times lower for Johnson & Johnson's vaccine).
Response, status and uncertainty
A phase 1 trial for a Bundibugyo-specific vaccine was launched by the University of Oxford. The Merck vaccine is available through the global Ebola vaccine stockpile and could be evaluated during the ongoing outbreak. The findings are not proof of protection but are encouraging, prompting calls for WHO to consider deploying Zaire vaccine stockpiles despite limited time for perfect evidence.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

埃博拉病毒病 后神经系统并发症持续数年

Neurological Complications Persist Years After Ebola Virus Disease
新闻简报《JAMA Neurology》发表的一项研究表明,埃博拉病毒病 幸存者在感染后 7 年多会经历持续的神经系统并发症。来自利比里亚 PREVAIL III 队列研究 的研究人员发现,虽然神经系统症状通常会随着时间的推移而改善,但与对照组相比,幸存者中记忆丧失、易怒和注意力不集中等认知缺陷仍然明显更为常见。这项研究包括 148 名幸存者和 81 名接触者,确定了严重的急性神经系统表现、较高的基线抗体滴度以及升高的 D-二聚体浓度与更大的长期神经系统负担之间的关联。这些发现强调 埃博拉病毒病 是一种潜在的神经营养性疾病,具有持久的神经系统影响,强调需要针对神经系统保护的治疗方法。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026 年 7 月 27 日。事件期间:研究于 2015 年 9 月至 2023 年 3 月期间进行,基线神经学评估平均为症状出现后 398 天,最终评估为急性感染后 7 年多。
地点与对象
人群:148 名成年 埃博拉病毒病 幸存者(平均年龄 34.8 岁;50% 为女性)和 81 名抗体阴性密切接触者(平均年龄 35.8 岁;51%),作为埃博拉自然史研究 (PREVAIL III;NCT02431923) 神经学研究的一部分。
事件
埃博拉病毒病 幸存者中已确认存在长期神经系统后遗症,认知缺陷在感染后持续 7 年以上。这项发表在《美国医学会神经病学》杂志上的研究发现,虽然神经系统症状通常会随着时间的推移而改善,但与对照组相比,幸存者记忆丧失、易怒和注意力不集中的情况仍然明显更为常见。
规模、影响与风险
规模:148 名幸存者和 81 名对照者。在基线时,幸存者报告记忆丧失(56.1% vs 17.3%)、抑郁症状(49.3% vs 16.0%)、注意力受损(43.2% vs 8.6%)和其他神经系统症状的发生率显着升高。在最终随访(>7 年)中,57.4% 的幸存者持续丧失记忆,而对照组为 26.2%。风险:埃博拉病毒病 应被认为是一种潜在的神经疾病,具有持久的结果,强调需要采取保护神经系统的治疗干预措施。
应对、状态与不确定性
回应:前瞻性纵向 队列研究 研究,每 6 个月进行一次标准化神经系统检查。不确定性包括无法让神经科医生了解参与者的状态、潜在的回忆偏差、急性疾病严重程度的记录不完整、参与者流失以及缺乏标准化的精神评估,这些评估可能低估了抑郁、焦虑和创伤后应激症状。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Neurological Complications Persist Years After Ebola Virus Disease

News Brief
A study published in JAMA Neurology reveals that Ebola virus disease survivors experience persistent neurological complications more than 7 years after infection. Researchers from the PREVAIL III cohort in Liberia found that while neurological symptoms generally improve over time, cognitive deficits such as memory loss, irritability, and difficulty concentrating remain significantly more common among survivors compared to controls. The study, which included 148 survivors and 81 contacts, identified associations between severe acute neurological manifestations, higher baseline antibody titers, and elevated D-dimer concentrations with greater long-term neurological burden. These findings underscore Ebola virus disease as a potentially neurotropic condition with lasting neurological impacts, emphasizing the need for therapies targeting nervous system protection.
Original source text (English)

Neurological symptoms among Ebola virus disease survivors improve over time, but cognitive deficits remain elevated more than 7 years after infection. These findings were published in JAMA Neurology. Researchers examined the evolution of neurological symptoms and examination findings among adult survivors of Ebola virus disease over more than 7 years to characterize the persistence of neurological sequelae. The researchers analyzed data from the Neurology Study of the Ebola Natural History Study (PREVAIL III; ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT02431923), a prospective longitudinal cohort study conducted in Liberia that enrolled adult Ebola survivors and Ebola antibody-negative close contacts. Participants underwent standardized neurological examinations and symptom assessments by trained neurologists approximately every 6 months between September 2015 and March 2023. Neurological symptoms, examination findings, and composite neurological examination scores were compared between survivors and control participants after adjustment for age and sex. These and other findings demonstrate [Ebola virus disease] should be recognized as a potentially neurotropic disease with long-lasting outcomes, highlighting critical need for therapeutic interventions protecting the nervous system. The analysis included 148 Ebola antibody-positive survivors (mean age, 34.8 years; 50% women) and 81 antibody-negative close contacts (mean age, 35.8 years; 51% women). The groups were generally similar with respect to age, sex distribution, educational attainment, body mass index, blood pressure, and most baseline medical conditions. Uveitis, however, was substantially more common among survivors than control participants (60.8% vs 30.8%; P <.001). Most survivors remained in Ebola treatment units for more than 14 days during acute illness. Overall, 68.9% reported acquiring the infection from a household or family member. Baseline neurological evaluations were conducted a mean of 398 days after symptom onset. At baseline neurological evaluation, the survivors reported significantly more ongoing neurological symptoms than the control participants. Compared with control participants, survivors were significantly more likely to report memory loss (56.1% vs 17.3%), depressive symptoms (49.3% vs 16.0%), impaired concentration (43.2% vs 8.6%), muscle soreness (35.8% vs 7.4%), reduced motivation (33.1% vs 4.9%), sexual dysfunction (32.4% vs 3.7%), and sensory abnormalities (30.4% vs 13.6%; all P <.001). Survivors also had significantly poorer sleep quality than control participants. Further, survivors had significantly higher overall and central nervous system examination scores, with abnormalities affecting both central and peripheral nervous system domains, including cranial neuropathies, motor weakness, sensory abnormalities, and cerebellar dysfunction. Neurological outcomes generally improved during long-term follow-up. By the final evaluation, conducted more than 7 years after acute infection, neurological examination scores were no longer significantly different between survivors and control participants. Despite these improvements, several cognitive symptoms remained significantly more common among survivors. Memory loss persisted in 57.4% of survivors compared with 26.2% of control participants (P <.001), irritability affected 36.5% vs 14.8% (P =.006), and difficulty concentrating was reported by 29.6% vs 9.8% (P =.002), respectively. Although headaches, fatigue, and depressive symptoms remained relatively common among survivors, these differences were no longer statistically significant at the final follow-up visit. In additional analyses, the researchers found that higher baseline Ebola antibody titers were associated with poorer neurological examination scores and a greater number of ongoing neurological symptoms. Survivors who experienced severe neurological manifestations during acute infection also had significantly higher neurological examination scores and greater symptom burden during follow-up. Elevated D-dimer concentrations and baseline memory complaints likewise were associated with a higher number of persistent neurological symptoms. Study limitations include the inability to blind neurologists to participants’ survivor status, the potential for recall bias when reporting historical symptoms, incomplete documentation of acute illness severity, participant attrition during long-term follow-up, and the absence of standardized psychiatric assessments, which may have resulted in underestimation of depression, anxiety, and post-traumatic stress symptoms. “These and other findings demonstrate [Ebola virus disease] should be recognized as a potentially neurotropic disease with long-lasting outcomes, highlighting critical need for therapeutic interventions protecting the nervous system,” the study authors concluded. They added, “Further research into the pathophysiologic mechanisms underlying these neurological findings is needed.” Disclosures: This research was supported by the National Institutes of Allergy and Infectious Diseases, the National Institute of Neurological Disorders and Stroke, and the National Cancer Institute. One study author declared affiliations with biotech, pharmaceutical, and/or device companies. Please see the original reference for a full list of disclosures. This article originally appeared on Neurology Advisor References: Billioux BJ, Reilly C, van Ryn C, et al. Neurological manifestations in adult survivors of Ebola virus disease. JAMA Neurol. View five news elements
Time
Publication date: July 27, 2026. Event period: Study conducted between September 2015 and March 2023, with baseline neurological evaluation a mean of 398 days after symptom onset and final evaluation more than 7 years after acute infection.
Location and population
Population: 148 adult Ebola virus disease survivors (mean age 34.8 years; 50% women) and 81 antibody-negative close contacts (mean age 35.8 years; 51%) as part of the Neurology Study of the Ebola Natural History Study (PREVAIL III; NCT02431923).
Event
Confirmed long-term neurological sequelae among Ebola virus disease survivors, with cognitive deficits persisting more than 7 years after infection. The study, published in JAMA Neurology, found that while neurological symptoms generally improved over time, memory loss, irritability, and difficulty concentrating remained significantly more common among survivors compared to controls.
Scale, impact and risk
Scale: 148 survivors and 81 controls. At baseline, survivors reported significantly higher rates of memory loss (56.1% vs 17.3%), depressive symptoms (49.3% vs 16.0%), impaired concentration (43.2% vs 8.6%), and other neurological symptoms. At final follow-up (>7 years), memory loss persisted in 57.4% of survivors vs 26.2% of controls. Risk: Ebola virus disease should be recognized as a potentially neurotropic disease with long-lasting outcomes, highlighting the need for therapeutic interventions protecting the nervous system.
Response, status and uncertainty
Response: Prospective longitudinal cohort study with standardized neurological examinations every 6 months. Uncertainties include inability to blind neurologists to participant status, potential recall bias, incomplete documentation of acute illness severity, participant attrition, and absence of standardized psychiatric assessments, which may have underestimated depression, anxiety, and post-traumatic stress symptoms.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

乌干达宣布 2026 年埃博拉疫情结束 暴发 - 乌干达媒体中心

UGANDA DECLARES THE END OF THE 2026 EBOLA DISEASE OUTBREAK - Uganda Media Centre
新闻简报乌干达卫生部正式宣布2026年埃博拉疫情暴发结束。该声明是在成功完成 42 天强制性监测期且没有新增确诊病例之后发布的。这一成就归功于政府强大的公共卫生系统、一线卫生工作者的奉献、社区的警惕和合作伙伴的支持。虽然该国目前已无埃博拉病毒且对所有活动开放,但卫生部敦促公众,特别是边境社区的公众,保持警惕并立即报告任何可疑症状。这标志着乌干达响应 暴发 的一个重要里程碑。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-28。事件日期:未明确说明,但42天的监测期于该日期完成。
地点与对象
乌干达,特别重视边境社区。
事件
乌干达在成功完成 42 天强制监测期且没有新增确诊病例后,正式宣布 2026 年埃博拉疫情 暴发 结束。这是卫生部报告的已证实的事件。
规模、影响与风险
42天监测期内无新增确诊病例。暴发 宣告结束。风险被明确表述为低,该国被宣布为无埃博拉病毒并开放商业、旅游业和所有社会经济活动。
应对、状态与不确定性
政府赞扬卫生工作者、社区、合作伙伴和所有乌干达人为遏制 暴发 所做的集体努力。敦促公众保持警惕,如果出现症状,请立即就医。来源是乌干达媒体中心的完整原始报道。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

UGANDA DECLARES THE END OF THE 2026 EBOLA DISEASE OUTBREAK - Uganda Media Centre

News Brief
The Ministry of Health has officially declared the end of the 2026 Ebola Disease outbreak in Uganda. This declaration follows the successful completion of the mandatory 42-day monitoring period with no new confirmed cases. The achievement is attributed to the Government's strong public health systems, the dedication of frontline health workers, community vigilance, and partner support. While the country is now Ebola-free and open for all activities, the Ministry urges the public, especially those in border communities, to remain vigilant and report any suspected symptoms immediately. This marks a significant milestone in Uganda's response to the outbreak.
Original source text (English)

The Government commends health workers, communities, partners, and all Ugandans for their collective efforts in containing the outbreak. The public is urged to remain vigilant and immediately seek medical attention if they experience symptoms such as fever, vomiting, diarrhoea, or unexplained bleeding. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

扎伊尔-埃博拉病毒-Impfstoff dazu geeignet 是否是 zugelassene,是不是本迪布焦-埃博拉病毒-Ausbruch zu bekämpfen?

Ist der zugelassene Zaire-Ebolavirus-Impfstoff dazu geeignet, den aktuellen Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Ausbruch zu bekämpfen?
新闻简报刚果民主共和国和乌干达正在发生记录最大规模的本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 暴发 疫情,共有 2,124 例确诊病例和 828 例死亡。该菌株尚无批准的治疗方法或 疫苗,这使得 暴发 反应变得复杂。然而,德国灵长类动物中心的研究表明,来自已批准的扎伊尔埃博拉病毒 疫苗 (VSV-ZEBOV-GP) 的抗体可能会抑制本迪布焦病毒进入人体细胞,这表明潜在的交叉保护。目前的病毒变种在感染人类细胞方面似乎并不比以前的病毒株更有效。这一发现可能有助于部署现有的 疫苗 以减轻 暴发 的影响,但还需要进一步的临床验证。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026-07-23。事件期间:2026年5月至2026年7月17日(持续进行中)。
地点与对象
受影响地区:刚果民主共和国东北部和邻国乌干达。目标人群:有本迪布焦埃博拉病毒感染风险的个人。
事件
确认了本迪布焦埃博拉病毒的 暴发,这是迄今为止记录到的该物种中最大的 暴发。研究表明,已批准的扎伊尔埃博拉病毒 疫苗 (VSV-ZEBOV-GP) 具有潜在的交叉保护作用。
规模、影响与风险
病例:2,124 例确诊感染。死亡人数:828 人死亡。风险:高,正在使用 传播,并且没有批准的治疗药物或针对本迪布焦埃博拉病毒的 疫苗。
应对、状态与不确定性
调查正在进行中;没有经批准的 Bundibugyo 特定 治疗 或 疫苗 可用。研究表明,扎伊尔埃博拉病毒 疫苗 抗体具有潜在的部分保护作用。来源限制:未审查完整的原始文章正文(content_status:syndicated_summary)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Ist der zugelassene Zaire-Ebolavirus-Impfstoff dazu geeignet, den aktuellen Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Ausbruch zu bekämpfen?

News Brief
The largest documented outbreak of Bundibugyo ebolavirus, with 2,124 confirmed cases and 828 deaths, is ongoing in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda. No approved therapeutics or vaccines exist for this strain, complicating outbreak response. However, research from the German Primate Center indicates that antibodies from the approved Zaire ebolavirus vaccine (VSV-ZEBOV-GP) may inhibit Bundibugyo virus entry into human cells, suggesting potential cross-protection. The current virus variant does not appear more efficient at infecting human cells than previous strains. This finding could aid in deploying existing vaccines to mitigate the outbreak's impact, though further clinical validation is needed.
Original source text (English)

Home > Press release: Ist der zugelassene ... News Ticker Magazine Events Experts Pictures Videos d c idw-News App: Back Similar press releases d Instance: Share Share on: 07/24/2026 14:59 Ist der zugelassene Zaire-Ebolavirus-Impfstoff geeignet, den aktuellen Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Ausbruch zu bekämpfen? Karin Tilch M.A. Stabsstelle Kommunikation Deutsches Primatenzentrum GmbH - Leibniz-Institut für Primatenforschung Forschende vom Deutschen Primatenzentrum – Leibniz-Institut für Primatenforschung in Göttingen liefern Hinweise auf einen möglichen Kreuzschutz durch einen bereits zugelassenen Zaire-Ebolavirus-Impfstoff. Ebolaviren zählen zu den gefährlichsten bekannten Krankheitserregern des Menschen. Drei Ebolavirus-Spezies haben wiederholt größere Krankheitsausbrüche auf dem afrikanischen Kontinent verursacht. Die meisten Ausbrüche wurden durch das Zaire-Ebolavirus ausgelöst. Für diese Virusart stehen Medikamente und Impfstoffe zur Verfügung. Gegen das Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus, den Auslöser des laufenden Ausbruchs in der Demokratischen Republik Kongo und Uganda, existieren hingegen bislang aber keine Therapeutika oder Impfstoffe. Dies erschwert erheblich die Bekämpfung des Ausbruchs, der bereits jetzt der größte jemals dokumentierte Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Ausbruch ist. Bisher war unklar, ob die aktuell zirkulierende Virusvariante effizienter in menschliche Zellen eindringen kann als frühere Varianten und damit zur ungewöhnlichen Dynamik des Ausbruchs beiträgt. Ebenso ungeklärt war die Frage, ob zugelassene Impfstoffe gegen das Zaire-Ebolavirus zumindest einen teilweisen Schutz gegen das Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus bewirken können. Ein Forschungsteam am Deutschen Primatenzentrum – Leibniz-Institut für Primatenforschung (DPZ) untersuchte beide Fragestellungen gemeinsam mit Forschenden des TWINCORE – Zentrum für Experimentelle und Klinische Infektionsforschung, sowie des Universitätsklinikums Hamburg-Eppendorf und der Charité – Universitätsmedizin Berlin. Dabei fanden die Wissenschaftler*innen keinen Hinweis darauf, dass die aktuelle Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Variante menschliche Zellen effizienter infiziert. Das Eindringen des Virus in Zellen konnte durch Antikörper in Seren von Personen gehemmt werden, die mit dem Vektorimpfstoff VSV-ZEBOV-GP geimpft wurden. Die Studie liefert damit wichtige Hinweise darauf, dass ein bereits verfügbarer Impfstoff aktuell gefährdete Personen vor schwerer Erkrankung schützen und zur Bekämpfung des laufenden Ausbruchs beitragen könnte (The Lancet Infectious Diseases). Bisher größter dokumentierter Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Ausbruch Seit die ersten Infektionen mit dem Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus im Mai 2026 im Nordosten der Demokratischen Republik Kongo registriert wurden, hat sich das Virus weiter ausgebreitet und auch das Nachbarland Uganda erreicht. Nach Angaben der Weltgesundheitsorganisation (WHO) vom 17. Juli 2026 wurden bislang 2.124 Infektionen bestätigt; 828 Menschen sind an den Folgen der Erkrankung verstorben. Ein Ende der Ausbreitung ist derzeit nicht absehbar. „Bereits jetzt handelt es sich um den größten jemals dokumentierten Ausbruch dieser Ebolavirus-Spezies“, erklärt Stefan Pöhlmann, Leiter der Abteilung Infektionsbiologie am DPZ. Keine zugelassenen Medikamente und Impfstoffe gegen das Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus verfügbar Das Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus ist eine von vier Ebolavirus-Spezies, die beim Menschen Erkrankungen verursachen können. Wiederkehrende größere Ausbrüche wurden bislang vor allem durch das Zaire-Ebolavirus verursacht, darunter die westafrikanische Ebola-Epidemie von 2014 bis 2016 mit mehr als 28.000 Infektionen und über 11.000 Todesfällen. Gegen das Zaire-Ebolavirus stehen inzwischen zugelassene Impfstoffe und Medikamente zur Verfügung. Bei Infektionen mit dem Bundibugyo- oder Sudan-Ebolavirus fehlen solche Präparate. Ihre Entwicklung und klinische Prüfung wird noch Zeit in Anspruch nehmen, was die Bekämpfung des aktuellen Ausbruchs erheblich erschwert. Keine Hinweise auf höhere Infektiosität der Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Variante Da die Zaire-Ebolavirus-Variante, die die bisher größte Ebola-Epidemie in Westafrika von 2014 bis 2016 auslöste, eine Mutation im Hüllprotein hatte, die wahrscheinlich das Eindringen in menschliche Zellen erleichterte, untersuchten die Forschenden zunächst, ob die aktuelle Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Variante leichter in menschliche Zellen eindringt als frühere Varianten. Für ihre Untersuchungen stellten die Forschenden am Deutschen Primatenzentrum sogenannte Pseudoviren her, das sind vermehrungsunfähige Viruspartikel, die mit den Hüllproteinen verschiedener Ebolaviren ausgestattet wurden. Diese wurden anschließend zur Infektion von Makrophagen und dendritische Zellen verwendet, die die wichtigsten Zielzellen der Ebolaviren sind. Die Untersuchungen ergaben keinen Hinweis auf eine erhöhte Infektiosität der aktuell zirkulierenden Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Variante. Antikörper nach Impfung gegen das Zaire-Ebolavirus neutralisieren auch die aktuelle Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Variante Um zu untersuchen, ob zugelassene Impfstoffe gegen das Zaire-Ebolavirus auch Antikörper hervorrufen, die die derzeit zirkulierende Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Variante neutralisieren, mischten die Forschenden Pseudoviren mit Serumproben geimpfter Personen, die an einer vorangegangenen klinischen Studie zur Immunogenität und Verträglichkeit des VSV-ZEBOV-GP-Impfstoffs teilnahmen. Anschließend analysierten sie, inwieweit die enthaltenen Antikörper den Eintritt der Pseudoviren in menschliche Zellen hemmten. Es zeigte sich, dass die meisten Proben Antikörper enthielten, die Pseudoviren mit dem Hüllprotein der aktuellen Bundibugyo-Ebolavirus-Variante neutralisierten, allerdings weniger effizient als Pseudoviren mit dem Zaire-Ebolavirus-Hüllprotein. Contact for scientific information:Dr. Markus Hoffmann Tel.: +49 (0) 551 3841-338 E-Mail: mhoffmann@dpz.eu Prof. Dr. Stefan Pöhlmann Tel.: +49 (0) 551 3851-150 E-Mail: spoehlmann@dpz.eu Original publication:Hoffmann M, Shandheep A, Pavlou A et al. Pseudovirus-based analysis of host cell entry and vaccine-induced neutralisation of Bundibugyo virus. The Lancet Infectious Diseases, 2026; https://www.thelancet.com/journals/laninf/article/PIIS1473-3099(26)00407-X/fullt... B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

CDC 有关埃博拉病毒的资源可协助医护人员做好 治疗 准备 - 美国医院协会

CDC resource on Ebola to assist healthcare workers with treatment preparation - American Hospital Association
新闻简报美国医院协会报告称,美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 发布了一种名为“埃博拉微学习”的新资源,以帮助美国医护人员为潜在的埃博拉病例做好准备。该培训模块强化了关键协议,包括患者评估、症状识别和感染控制措施。CDC强调,美国尚未确认与刚果民主共和国和乌干达正在发生的暴发有关的埃博拉病例,目前美国民众不存在风险。该资源是主动准备工作的一部分,旨在确保医疗机构准备好在需要时安全有效地做出响应。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 28 日。事件日期:未明确说明,但资源发布被称为最近发布。
地点与对象
目标人群:准备治疗疑似埃博拉病患者的一线医护人员。
事件
美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 发布了名为“埃博拉微学习”的资源,以帮助医护人员为治疗疑似埃博拉疾病患者做好准备和培训。这是确认的指导,而不是 暴发 报告。
规模、影响与风险
美国尚未确认刚果民主共和国和乌干达当前埃博拉病毒 暴发 病例。CDC 表示,目前该 暴发 对美国民众不构成风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 发布此资源作为加强准备的预防措施。该资源强化了评估旅行史、识别症状、遵循“识别、隔离和告知”方法以及实施感染预防措施等概念。消息来源是美国医院协会援引疾病预防控制中心的一份正式通知。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

CDC resource on Ebola to assist healthcare workers with treatment preparation - American Hospital Association

News Brief
The American Hospital Association reports that the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) has released a new resource called 'Ebola Micro-Learn' to help U.S. healthcare workers prepare for potential Ebola cases. This training module reinforces key protocols including patient assessment, symptom recognition, and infection control measures. The CDC emphasizes that no cases of Ebola have been confirmed in the United States related to the ongoing outbreaks in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda, and there is currently no risk to the U.S. population. This resource is part of proactive preparedness efforts to ensure healthcare facilities are ready to respond safely and effectively if needed.
